| 2026-07-12 | Failure-atomic KV pool replacement foundation | `sha256:f07b09f0334a` | this commit | portable; Strix Halo ROCm/Vulkan compile gates | 379 model library tests; focused pool identity, shrink, grow, copy, zero-tail, and no-op tests; default, ROCm gfx1151, and Vulkan server all-target checks; formatting and diff hygiene | passed | Every paged-KV cache now has a process-unique allocation ID and a generation that advances only after a successful physical replacement. Shrink and grow stage every layer, preserve the old tensors and published capacity through allocation/copy/final-synchronization failure, then perform one pool/capacity/generation commit. No-op resize preserves identity, separately constructed pools cannot alias identity, and successful shrink/grow preserve the KV prefix while zeroing the grown tail. This intentionally replaces the old host-staged shrink's low transient high-water with an `old + new` headroom requirement: portable failure atomicity is the correctness priority, and the autoscaler must account for staging headroom before the device gate can pass. Graph replay binding, actor-level `BlockManager` publication, fault injection, and real ROCm acceptance remain open, so no Phase 1.2 checkbox is closed here. |
| 2026-07-12 | Paged-KV graph allocation identity binding | `sha256:7bf11e90199a` | this commit | portable; ROCm/Vulkan compile; toolkit-free CUDA compile | 379 model library tests; 22 resource-concurrency invariants; focused stale/current identity rejection; default, ROCm gfx1151, Vulkan, and `CUDARC_CUDA_VERSION=12080` CUDA checks; formatting and diff hygiene | passed | CUDA single-row and opt-in batched graphs plus ROCm HIP graphs now retain the exact paged-cache allocation ID, generation, and block count captured by their kernels. Every replay compares live identity before any native launch; a mismatch destroys that cached graph and takes eager fallback rather than touching stale pointers. The production physical-resize route additionally acquires the exclusive GPU barrier and model write lock, checks backend health, and invalidates CUDA, ROCm, and Metal decode graph state before the cache can replace or release an allocation. The cache-level guard proves an old generation is rejected while the current generation remains accepted. Actor drain, transactional logical-capacity publication, injected failures, and real graph-before/after-resize hardware proof remain open, so this is defense-in-depth implementation evidence rather than Phase 1.2 acceptance. |
| 2026-07-12 | Drained FIFO KV resize barrier | `sha256:38fbc4d35646` | this commit | portable; ROCm/Vulkan compile; toolkit-free CUDA compile | 914 server library tests; deterministic active-resize-admission ordering and existing adapter-order regressions; default, ROCm gfx1151, Vulkan, and `CUDARC_CUDA_VERSION=12080` CUDA server all-target checks; formatting and diff hygiene | passed | Physical resize no longer runs merely between two actor steps. Resize and adapter swaps share one FIFO-exclusive mutation queue; a pending mutation pauses admission, lets every active prefill/decode row finish, and runs only at the between-requests barrier. A gated two-step request proves resize cannot fire while active, the queued request cannot prepare early, both decode steps precede resize, and admission resumes only afterward. The production route then holds the global GPU writer and model writer, invalidates graphs, prepares a cloned logical `BlockManager`, commits the physical pool transaction, and publishes the logical state with one infallible assignment. A physical error therefore cannot leave a pre-published logical shrink or grow. Direct inference workers retain the shared GPU read owner through cleanup, so the same writer excludes their live accelerator ownership. This closes the quiescent-barrier implementation item; injected boundary failures and current-source ROCm resize/graph evidence remain required before accepting transactional resize as a whole. |

| 2026-07-12 | Exhaustive KV resize rollback proof | `sha256:14d216460385` | this commit | portable; ROCm/Vulkan compile; toolkit-free CUDA compile | 104 core tests with 3 ignored; 382 model and 918 server library tests; 42 injected shrink/grow transaction failures plus no-op, logical rollback, successful publication, and capacity-mismatch regressions; default, ROCm, Vulkan, and `CUDARC_CUDA_VERSION=12080` CUDA server all-target checks; formatting and diff hygiene | passed | The physical transaction exposes a unique deterministic checkpoint before the initial synchronization; each key and value allocation; each zero-fill synchronization; each key and value prefix copy; every staged-layer boundary; the final synchronization; and the single commit. Three layers produce 21 distinct checkpoints per direction. Injecting every shrink and grow failure preserves exact pool tensor identities, dimensions, values, allocation identity, generation, and block count, and every failed cache remains retryable. Production logical publication now uses the same tested transaction helper: a cloned `BlockManager` is assigned only after physical success, injected shrink/grow failures leave it exactly unchanged, a successful operation publishes matching logical/physical capacity, and a preexisting mismatch fails before physical work. This closes portable failure atomicity and fault injection; transient `old + new` headroom accounting plus current-source ROCm eager/captured resize execution remain required before Phase 1.2 acceptance. |

| 2026-07-12 | Transactional KV staging-headroom policy | `sha256:bc885feaa270` | this commit | portable; ROCm/Vulkan compile; toolkit-free CUDA compile | 923 server library tests; 10 autoscaler staging, direction, floor, step, hysteresis, and bounded-backoff regressions; training-reclamation staging regression; default, ROCm, Vulkan, and `CUDARC_CUDA_VERSION=12080` CUDA server all-target checks; formatting and diff hygiene | passed | Every production resize caller now budgets the full replacement pool while the old generation remains live; grow is no longer incorrectly admitted from delta-only headroom. Policy decisions use the stricter governor/allocator signal, while staging uses the backend allocator's actual safe bytes and includes reusable ROCm pool spare. A typed plan may cap grow, may deepen an automatic shrink only within its explicit step/floor, and refuses an exact forced shrink rather than silently crossing the operator target. Accepted plans hold a temporary governor reservation for all replacement bytes through the drained physical transaction. Training reclamation separately tracks required bytes and the exact capped reservation it actually holds, preserves unrelated reservations, and may deepen to the smallest stageable target above its one-block floor. Insufficient headroom, allocation failure, and zero-yield resize retry with bounded 8-128 second exponential backoff instead of a two-second loop. Current-source real ROCm shrink/grow and graph replay evidence remain required. |

| 2026-07-12 | ROCm replacement-tail corruption fix | `sha256:daa06785a1f0` | `2f448a2d` | Strix Halo ROCm/gfx1151; portable; backend compile matrix | five consecutive 2 GB physical shrink/reuse/grow qualifications; 1 GB independent shrink proof; 11 ROCm storage smoke tests with 2 ignored; 382 model, 987 tensor, 6 HIP, and 923 server library tests; default, ROCm, Vulkan, and `CUDARC_CUDA_VERSION=12080` toolkit-free CUDA server all-target checks; hosted CI run `29207419461` | passed | The clean transactional implementation reproducibly copied a marker into the grown layer-0 key pool and later returned zeros after additional staged allocations touched the multi-gigabyte replacement generation. A bounds-checked raw range D2H path removed the temporary device allocation from compact-view observation and localized the loss to replacement staging rather than readback. ROCm replacement pools now allocate uninitialized, copy the complete surviving prefix before commit, and leave grown free blocks inaccessible until block assignment plus their mandatory first KV write; constructors remain zeroed and other backends retain zeroed resize tails. This removes a needless multi-gigabyte mid-resize write and its observed corruption mode. Every repeated run preserved the marker through nine 200 MiB training-like allocations, restored 8,000 blocks, and round-tripped the first grown slot after write. Hosted CI passed formatting, cargo-deny, default build/tests, substrate tests, and qualification-tool validation while expensive GPU lanes remained skipped by policy. This is the physical-substrate evidence paired with the serving-path runtime proof below. |

| 2026-07-12 | ROCm stale-generation native-launch refusal | `sha256:5dfdf3a71f2f` | this commit | Strix Halo ROCm/gfx1151; portable; unit-build launch instrumentation | exact ignored `kiln-model` qualification; six consecutive hardware runs; 382 portable model tests; default and ROCm model all-target checks | passed | The test establishes one valid capture and native replay, computes the next eager oracle on the same cache, and corrupts only the graph's retained pool generation. Every outlier trace also carries the closed attribution class and full nearby category set. Regression tests prove that an outlier overlapping both actor work and thermal pacing is classified as thermal, while memory reclaim, synchronization, or actor-only attribution remains non-thermal and fails the gate. The mixed-load client deadline is now 180 seconds, exactly matching its existing server request containment bound; all cooling time remains in TTFT, E2E, workload duration, and sustainable throughput. The first complete test run caught two shared-helper couplings before commit: ROCm development soaks had inherited the mixed driver's 120-second constant, and Vulkan baseline, development soak, and graph-resilience callers unpacked the old classifier tuple. The soak now owns and retains its explicit 120-second contract, and every caller consumes the typed result fields without widening any unrelated phase or case deadline. No hardware acceptance is claimed until the clean pushed-source `both-off` arm completes the fixed 1,184-token denominator under this exact tree. |

## Known Starting Defects

These are anchors, not an exhaustive substitute for the checklist:

- ROCm pool trim can synchronize the device every two seconds under pressure:
  `crates/kiln-memory/src/governor.rs`, `crates/kiln-server/src/state.rs`, and
  `crates/kiln-hip/src/lib.rs`.
- Default KV autoscaling can resize/copy live cache capacity between decode
  steps: `crates/kiln-server/src/kv_autoscaler.rs`,
  `crates/kiln-server/src/batching_engine.rs`, and
  `crates/kiln-model/src/paged_kv_cache_kt.rs`.
- ROCm captured graphs omit KV pool generation/identity and are not invalidated
  by physical resize: `crates/kiln-model/src/rocm_graph.rs` and
  `crates/kiln-model/src/generate.rs`.
- A full response channel can sleep the compute actor for up to two seconds:
  `crates/kiln-server/src/batching_engine.rs`.
- GRPO uses one reference tensor for both importance ratio and KL while rollout
  provenance lacks behavior log-probabilities: `crates/kiln-train/src/lib.rs`,
  `crates/kiln-train/src/trajectory.rs`, and
  `crates/kiln-train/src/trainer.rs`.
- Resolved in Phases 4.1 and 4.2: eval `n > 1` aggregated completions rather
  than examples, and synthesis could label training rows as held out:
  `crates/kiln-eval/src/suite.rs`,
  `crates/kiln-eval/src/result.rs`, `crates/kiln-eval/src/synthesis.rs`, and
  `docs/site/evals.html`.
- Thinking-budget configuration, streaming logs, batch/compare surfaces, recent
  requests, and desktop semantics are not yet one conformance-tested contract:
  `crates/kiln-server/src/config.rs`,
  `crates/kiln-server/src/request_log.rs`,
  `crates/kiln-server/src/api/completions.rs`,
  `crates/kiln-server/src/ui/app.js`, and `desktop/`.
